In principle, intensifications have been introduced in the direction that there is a ceiling of donations with a penalty mechanism. As far as the activity report is concerned, a breakdown of the political parties, a clarification has been made – i.e. also a demand that stood in the room. In this sense, the Valorisation Rules are also completed, and grants up to EUR 100 per person and event for local political events, provided that they are not currently subject to the registration fee obligation, are also not covered by the donation concept. Thus, it will continue to be possible to conduct political events with a municipal political character such as summer festivals, barbeques, children's fascists and others in a low and unbureaucratic framework. In doing so, the border is deliberately so low as to exclude any influence on political decisions. Fatally, the Court of Auditors has always been given the impression that there is hardly any possibility to review the reports of the political parties. This is wrong, and I would like to explain that too – my colleague Matznetter mentioned it earlier. Each party proposes five auditors for its accounting report. Two auditors are drawn from the five proposals from the Court of Auditors. These are subject to strict regulation: They are subject to the economic loyalty regulation and must exercise their profession in full compliance with the laws and confidentiality of their own responsibility and with full responsibility for their activities, governed by directives. In the absence of confidence, they may be deprived of powers. What is the Court of Auditors doing? If he does not believe an indication of a party, he has the opportunity to request information, he has the task of re-examining and testing his two nominees from the five proposed auditors. And if he still has doubts, he can also appoint an independent auditor who checks everything. This is also the practice of the Court of Auditors, away from the public, which is simply never colported. [[Conjugation with the SPÖ and the Abg. Stefan.]] Therefore, this impression that the Court of Auditors has no powers of examination is completely wrong. ParlaMint-AT_2019-07-03-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00086_d8e10183	Madam President. Mr. Minister of Finance! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Dear Seers and Seers! Before the voting marathon on the subject of party law, we still devote ourselves to a slightly less stressful but equally important subject, namely the 2. Service law Novelle 2019. This service law novel provides for the dissolution of the Disciplinary Commissions in the Ministries, of which there are at least 30 and about a hundred Senates in which decision will be made. Instead, it is intended to create its own Federal Disciplinary Authority, which is then responsible for all federal officials. This is necessary because, on the one hand, the number of officials continues to decline, on the other hand, there are also fewer and fewer disciplinary procedures, but if they exist, then they will ever more complex. In order to increase quality, it is therefore good and important if such procedures are to be conducted centrally. However, it would have been difficult if this Federal Disciplinary Authority had also been responsible for the officials of the Parliamentary Directorate, the Court of Auditors and the Public Prosecutor's Office, for then, as it were, the administration would have controlled the legislation. This would have been problematic for the reasons of the division of violence, and that is why, with the addition of the Federal Constitutional Act for the officials of the Parliamentary Directorate, the Court of Auditors and the Public Prosecutors, a separate Disciplinary Commission will be set up. Another essential point, a problem that could be solved, is that of the day of preemption. The problem has been following us for over ten years, and it has been tried several times to solve this problem. In the case at last, the European Court of Justice held on 8 May. May 2019 states that the transfer to a new remuneration and pre-emption system from 2015 is illegal in Europe. Furthermore, the retroactive abolition of the pre-emptive date and the limitation of the accounting of professional times have been recognised as unlawful in Europe. This recognition of the European Court of Justice shall apply immediately as long as there is no European-law-based solution. The legal uncertainty resulting therefrom entails budgetary risks. It was therefore extremely urgent to establish a non-discrimination-free legal position in the interests of the Republic. With this innovation, we have reached a common solution to this problem. On the one hand, the pre-emptive day is no longer due to the 18th century. Set up a birthday. The school age will be uniform from 1st century onwards. ==References== School level counts, and the times charged in the public interest are now unlimited. Furthermore, in future, all forms of presence and civil service will be counted to the extent of the times actually completed. This will eliminate age discrimination and also the contradiction to the free movement of workers. The costs for this amount to around 60 million euros per year and one single 165 million euros for additional payments. But they are in absolutely no relation to the costs that could have been incurred without this regulation that we have made. That is why I would like to thank Mr Eduard Müller very much for finding this solution in such a short time. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] In the last committee, a joint ÖVP FPÖ-SPÖ application was submitted calling on the Federal Government to allow judicial guards with particularly taxing activities access to the heavy-work pension. It is a recognition of this difficult, personal, and dangerous activity that our judicial officers are exercising in all our interests in order to ensure our security. They have to do with people who sit behind bars for a variety of reasons every day, people who are often not tender and even delicate. I myself have visited some judicial institutions in Austria in recent months. The work there is not easy. ParlaMint-AT_2019-07-03-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00086_d8e11972	We now come to points 19 and 20 of the agenda on which the debate is being held under one. There was no oral reporting. Mr Jan Krainer is the first to speak. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2019-07-03-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00086_d8e11999	Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen! This will be a somewhat more complicated speech because we are dealing with a somewhat more complicated matter. At the end of the day, it must be said that ten days ago we received a request from the former government parties. This application had slim 155 pages, of which 91 pages were standard text. We have worked through and severed the request, and I can report on the results here. [[President Kitzmüller shall take over the chairmanship.]] What happens is that, on the one hand, a number of directives are being implemented by the European Union and, on the other, a regulation is considered to be "accompanied by law". A directive always provides a framework, and then we translate it into national law. A regulation works directly, but usually requires certain laws to accompany it. The first directive to be implemented here is Directive 2017/1852 on EU procedures for the settlement of tax disputes. It is about the fact that, when two EU countries have a conflict – where a company or a person has to pay their taxes or what taxes to pay them – there is a single set of rules which states in what time frame, in a maximum period of two years, and in what form this dispute should be resolved. That is good, we agree. In this law, I believe, there are 19 articles. Articles 1, 3 and 5 implement this directive, and these find the approval of us Social Democrats. The next one to be implemented here is Directive 2017/1371. This concerns the fight against criminal law against fraud against the Union's financial interests, particularly VAT fraud. It is true that the punishments are united. So far, there are different penalties in different countries. As a general rule, this means an increase in penalties, in exceptional situations, which according to the Ministry of Finance only on paper and not to exist in reality, there is a reduction. This is the so-called commercial tax fraud, which the officials of the Ministry of Finance believe is undetectable in practice. That is, in practice, there is only one increase. The board of attorneys has just complained that this commercial fraud is intended to reduce punishment and not increase it. The officials of the Ministry of Finance believe that this is only theoretical, in practice it has no effect. This concerns Article 4 of the proposal. . We agree here, even if there are concerns of the Bar. It also concerns Directive 2018/843 to prevent the use of the financial system for the purposes of money laundering and terrorist financing. This concerns Articles 16, 17 and 18 of the motion. We agree with Articles 17 and 18 that we see the following problem at 16. This is the financial market money laundering law, and § 2 defines what this law is all about. There are five sub-points – a, b, c, d and e – and points (a) and (b) are the transposition of directives, (c), (d) and (e) without wishing to go into detail now, in our view, there is no directive - at least we do not find it in the directive - but materially something else. One can discuss this, but we honestly didn't have the time to see exactly what c, d and e were concerned. That is, Article 16 will be rejected for this reason. The following is the EU Regulation 2017/1129 on the prospectus to be published in the public offer of securities or when admission to trading on a regulated market. The regulation works directly anyway, but the accompanying laws make a difference. This is essentially the so-called KMG – that is being lifted and re-captured, and there is a deterioration in consumer protection. It is about the deadlines within which someone can resign. That was infinite so far, in the draft assessment this was limited to five years, it has disappeared and it is again – under quotation marks – “infinite”, but in the case of further reports it is now seven days. This is a restriction on consumer protection which is not covered by the Regulation. That's what we're going to reject. The second criticism is that there are three bodies responsible for examining the prospectus and not one. Financial market supervision, the Austrian Bar Association and also we believe that an authority should be responsible – reasonably – for financial market supervision. However, we have three different bodies: FMA, District Administration and Brochure Controller. This is not mandatory from the regulation, so we will reject it. This concerns, in particular, Article 2, but in addition Articles 6, 7, 8, 9, 10, 11, 12, 14 and 15, which refer to this Article 2 and which we will then reject. We shall vote in favour of Article 13. This concerns occupational and self-employed pension funds, so that the so-called zero risk bonds are available not only from the ECB but also from local authorities. The latter, which is still contained, is the Insurance Supervision Act – there is a Treaty between the European Union and the USA on how insurances that work across the border are handled. We agree with Article 19. I know it wasn't totally comprehensible for everyone now, but it's a very complex matter. I do not blame anyone that this was so short-term, which is why our answer is so short-term, but it's difficult, I say, to say one- times to analyze these 155 pages in ten days. That's the analysis we have. I would also like to explain our voting behaviour and explain where we agree and what articles we reject. There will then be a series of separate votes on the basis of these articles which we reject. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2019-07-03-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00086_d8e12366	Madam President. High house! Mr. Minister! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) The fact that this change, this revision of the Stock Exchange Act 2018, is a very technological matter, has proved not only to my predecessors, but also to me. I would like to draw up two main objectives of the directive: Identification of shareholders and, on the other hand, transparency of institutional investors, asset managers and voting rights consultants. The aim is therefore to promote the long-term participation of shareholders and also transparency between shareholders, shareholders and investors. To this end, companies need to be able to identify their shareholders, so they can get directly in touch with them to communicate directly with them. This is happening with a view to facilitating the exercise of shareholder rights – that is what it is all about – and the cooperation of shareholders with the shareholders. This is mainly to be done by so-called intermediaries, which are investment firms, credit institutions and so on, i.e. those providing services for the storage, management of securities or the management of deposit accounts on behalf of shareholders and other persons. The point that the Committee has made for the biggest discussion was the establishment of the so-called threshold value, which the colleague, my previous speaker, Mr Rossmann, has also mentioned, at 0.5 percent. For the explanation only briefly: The company's right to identification, i.e. the right of the company to know who its shareholder is, shall not apply until the shareholder holds at least 0.5 percent of the shares or voting rights. The directive leaves a margin open, it would also be possible to set a lower threshold, e.g. 0.1 percent, but from our point of view such a scheme would be completely outrageous. It would create an administrative burden that cannot be justified by nothing. This administrative burden would blow up in an explosion-like way and would thus blow all borders. Therefore, from our point of view, it is simply fair to set this threshold at 0.5 percent, because we also avoid gold plating – I disagree with my previous speaker. Mr Finanzminister, you have confirmed in the Committee that the threshold has been set on the basis of an opinion based on expert assessments and also on the specific situation in Austria. Mr Rossmann, I would just like to tell you – and I give it to you only if you listen to me – that: [[in the direction of the with Abg. Krainer speaking Abg. Rossmann]] It also needs a majority in this House if you want to make a decision, and this majority has just reached 0.5 percent. High house! This novel creates transparency, it creates efficiency, it creates security and it creates more fairness, and that is why we will decide on it today. The year 2009 – it was already addressed – brought the launch under the then Finance Minister Josef Pröll. ==References== He proposed a so-called transfer account for each household on October 9, 2009, with the title Project Österreich. This should show the support of each household. months later, the then Federal Government decided on the transparency database, as we know it today. The aim of this amendment is to further exploit the potential of the transparency database in order to meet the objectives and purposes of the transparency database even more successfully. The economic, efficient and effective use of public funds is crucial for the public budget, with the transparency database acting as an important instrument for efficient funding. The control and efficiency of resource use and the further increase in transparency are important to us. The policy has the task of dealing with tax money carefully in all areas, whether at federal, national or local level. As a Upper Austrian, I am delighted that Oberösterreich, the first federal state, together with Lower Austria, voluntarily subscribes to the data beyond the mandatory level. There are, however, ladies and gentlemen, also federal states that refuse to disclose their data in the field of funding: the Burgenland, Carinthia and the Federal Capital of Vienna. Ladies and gentlemen of SPÖ, preach transparency and comprehensibleness, and what do your national leaders do? – They refuse the reports to the Federation that would increase transparency. All the federal states want to promote, but these three red-led federal states are not willing to announce who is referring to the funding. Is there a party tactic behind that? The amendment to be adopted today also contains further improvements concerning registrations into the system. Currently, registrations to the system must be made only at the time of payment. Today's decision links the notifications to the database to the grant of support. This significantly improves the quality of information, especially for the eligible funding agencies. In addition, it is now possible to see the status of the application for funding. This amendment eliminates criticisms raised in the joint analysis with the countries on the transparency database and implements numerous recommendations from the Court of Auditors. A great deal of thanks goes to all those involved under the then Finance Minister Löger and the current Finance Minister Müller, who were involved in it – in the sense of transparency, in the sense of the sparing handling of the tax money, in the sense of the Austrians who deserve it, to know what happens to their tax money. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2019-07-03-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00086_d8e15214	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Ladies and gentlemen! The non-profit sector is a very, very important sector in Austria's housing industry, and we take this into account. I absolutely cannot understand your remarks, ladies and gentlemen, which you have now made; Mr Steinacker will comment accordingly. Ladies and gentlemen! A recent study shows that 62 percent of 18- to 34-year-olds want home ownership. In order to make this desire easier to reality, we are improving the rental model within the framework of this new novel, namely the novel of the housing law on non-profit-making. This is the first focus of this innovation: facilitating ownership for tenants. How does this rental model work? – At the time of the rental you are first a tenant of an apartment and can buy it at a later time. The purchase price is, of course, lower than that on the free market. By facilitating the purchase of rents, we enable people who earn less to create themselves and – which is very important – nevertheless high-quality property. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] To clarify: With this reform, we do not change the price formation. What we change is the framework conditions under which rental takes place. On the one hand, we extend the period within which one can decide whether to buy the rented apartment. Whoever comes to his decision after a few years to want to buy the apartment better, because perhaps by now the financial possibilities have improved, can do so already after five years. In the future, it will also be possible to make a total of three requests for the purchase of the apartment during the extended 15-year period. So far, under the existing regulation, this was only possible once. With this law we also ensure that people receive better information about the rental purchase. In the lease contract, the tenant or the later owner must be clearly explained, under what conditions and under what conditions he is entitled to the purchase of the apartment. Better information and more applications over a longer period of time will in any case lead to more people choosing to buy rent. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] Ladies and gentlemen! Another focus of the amendment concerns the increased protection of non-profit housing vis-à-vis speculators. These have found legal loopholes in the past to get to non-profit objects and then sell them on a pricey basis. The aim of the reform is therefore to take all legal measures to ensure that non-profit housing remains non-profit in the future and that residents do not have to worry about the legal status of their home. Ladies and gentlemen! What we don't want to do with the rental sale is that someone acquires an apartment cheaply, but doesn't need it for their own needs, but sells it to others profitably or rents it on. We therefore introduce a 15-year speculation period for both sale and rental. If you sell your apartment at market value within 15 years, you will have to repay your profits to the public. If you rent, you can only demand a very strictly limited rental rate for 15 years. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and FPÖ.]] For those who are talking about alleged rental increases, I would like to briefly explain how social housing works. The common good can, indeed, be more favourable than private, because these apartments are built by means of housing, because they must not profit from the common good. Any euro that flows into the establishment, maintenance and improvement of housing, but will ultimately be paid by the residents in the form of monthly rents. Investments increasingly include environmental measures. I am thinking of resources for thermal renovation, for the construction of renewable energy production and supply plants, in particular photovoltaic plants. I am thinking that for electric vehicles, corresponding pipeline infrastructure is also being built for charging purposes, and so on. All of this, of course, costs money. Not only do the inhabitants rightly call for such measures for environmental protection, but the public are even obliged by law to provide modern housing. With this innovation, however, we are only making it possible to finance environmental measures. We also make it clear that, initially, the efficiency of projects must always be examined. Ladies and gentlemen! The non-profit housing industry – which I have already done – is an essential factor in Austria's housing landscape. A lot of European countries envy us about this institution. I am very grateful to the FPÖ, especially our colleague Philipp Schrangl, for your staff, Mrs. Federal Minister, at the Federal Ministry of Digitalization and Business Location, and for all those who have contributed to the development of this novelle. I am absolutely convinced that with this novel, we have put in place a truly comprehensive package in the sense of non-profit housing, and above all this will bring many improvements to more than a million Austrians living in non-profit housing. In this sense, I may ask you to agree to this amendment. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-AT_2019-07-03-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00086_d8e16220	Mr President. High house! Dear ladies and gentlemen at the audience gallery! Today's own-initiative proposal to the Novelle of the Law on Housing Genuineness, as already mentioned, is based on a draft assessment by the Ministry of Economic Affairs of April this year. The four main aspects of this novel are the same as in the draft ministerial opinion. Therefore, allow me to address these four main aspects! Point one relates to increased protection of non-profit-making assets. These are, in particular, the newly created opportunities for the State Supervisory Authorities, which may lead, for example, in the event of a risk delay from the establishment of a government commissioner to the acquisition of ownership of shares in a non-profit building association by the countries as Ultima Ratio. The introduction of more transparent rules for the remuneration of the managing directors of non-profit building associations should also be mentioned here, according to the so-called contract stencil regulation, as is otherwise customary for public enterprises. The second point concerns the facilitated formation of ownership for tenants. Instead of the previous legal situation in which an application was made between the eleventh and the end of the 15th century. The year of the rental contract was possible, there will be three possible applications for purchase each from the sixth, from the eleventh and between the 16th and the 20th years. Year. In addition, the tenants in the lease agreement must clearly refer to the rights they have. The third point concerns the safeguarding of the home housing stock. Compared to a previously free rental option by the acquiring tenant, renting should only be allowed for a certain period of time, i.e. within 15 years, only at the respective national legal value. This point has been included in order to prevent speculation and because the self-utilising and non- Speculative Property Owner is at the heart of a subsequent transfer of residential property. In addition, this novelle also includes incentives for more renovations, in terms of thermal renovations or renovations made in favour of accessibility, through increased own resources of the non-profit building association, without increasing the rents and with full verification of the rehabilitation measures carried out. Moreover, and this too has already been mentioned, Airbnb rentals will not be possible in the future, as the created housing is intended to serve the purpose of the residence and not the tourist purpose. The fourth point concerns the sustainability of the non-profit housing sector and in this context in particular the easier possibility of the construction of photovoltaic systems and the operation of such facilities. In addition, the role of non-profit associations as social housing providers has also been underlined in the example of victims of violence. That too is a very important point. These people will be able to have a preferred access to non-profit accommodation in the future. I am glad that the original draft still incorporates the comprehensive opinions from the review, and I hope that these contributions can also be widely accepted. Thank you.

In addition to CO2 emissions from transport, space heating is known to have a significant share of the national CO2 balance. Oil, which we burn in oil boilers, is a true climate change driver. We really need to stamp the burning of oil for heating purposes completely, and I therefore agree with the unsolicited motion for a resolution tabled by Mr Josef Lettenbichler, colleagues, that the budget for the 2019 reform offensive should be increased by 10 million euros, of course, and that is what I agree with. However, as Mr Rossmann said earlier, this is not enough. It was 42.7 million euros in the budget pot, which had already been exhausted in June. This shows a great demand and also the willingness of consumers to switch from climate-friendly oil to climate-friendly alternatives. The second unself-employed motion for a resolution tabled by Mr Bruno Rossmann is proposing a higher increase, namely 20 million euros, and I also agree with it. It is said to gain the better and stronger, and the stronger will then beat the weaker. Yes, in fact, even this increase by 20 million euros is not enough, because it takes 100 million euros annually. We still have 700 000 oil boilers in Austria – 700 000! For us to be able to say goodbye to these oil boilers quickly enough, it takes this 100 million euros a year. That sounds like a lot now, but I dare to think about it. What would happen if the climate-friendly subsidies in Austria amounting to 3.8 to 4.7 billion euros were transformed into incentives for climate-friendly behaviour? Alternative systems have long been technically and market-economy matured. For 20 years I have been in the energy transition industry, and I have had to hear again and again: Technically and economically, everything would be done, the energy turnaround would be possible in a very short time, but politics is still missing, it does not turn on the swivels yet. So we've committed ourselves. Austria has already committed itself years ago to making policy more environmentally friendly, and compliance with the 1.5-degree objective of the Paris climate agreement is an important goal for Austria. We do not want to pay billions of fines in 2030 either, because we have not complied with EU climate change targets. If you compare this 100 million euros a year with the billion-dollar fines and the climate-damaging subsidies of 4.7 billion euros a year, then you will find out where we should set priorities. It is really time to rethink and restructure the funding: away from climate damage to environmentally friendly subsidisation. I am also pleased, however, as the colleagues welcomed earlier, that we are still able to take some effective measures today and that The free play of the forces enables us to increase the budget for the oil boiler exchange. I am also pleased with the deadline proposal tabled today by Mr Lettenbichler regarding the request for a federal ban on oil boilers in new buildings. – I am glad too! Thank you very much for the attention and let us continue to work together for the energy transition and for climate protection in the country! This is not only what we see, but also Germany, Finland and Greece have already objected. The purpose of the opposition to the accession of the Philippines to this Hague Convention is to prevent documents originating in the Philippines from being used as evidence without further national control as to their authenticity or correctness in proceedings before domestic authorities. This means that in Austria we reserve ourselves to continue examining documents and documents from the Philippines for their authenticity, thus ensuring the necessary document security. The third and last items of the agenda, agenda item 26, are about the Protocol to the Convention on Forced Labour. The agreement itself dates back to 1930, with the 2014 Protocol including the modern impact of forced labour, such as trafficking in human beings and labour exploitation, and it has strengthened victim protection in particular. The present recommendation, which is in itself non-binding, has included further additional proposals. It has been looked at very closely in the ministries concerned and has been concluded that the Austrian legal situation is actually in line with or includes the proposals contained in these recommendations, which is why ratification and approval by Austria is possible. There is also a certain symbol character, because the ILO, the International Labour Organization, is today the 100. The anniversary of its foundation celebrates, and it would, of course, be a beautiful act if Austria took note of it and honoured it by the approval of this recommendation. – Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-AT_2019-07-03-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00086_d8e18946	Mrs Dr.in Jessi Lintl came to speak. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2019-07-03-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00086_d8e18967	Madam President. Mr. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! I refer to Agenda item 25 concerning the opposition of the Republic of Austria against the accession of the Republic of the Philippines to the Convention on the Liberation of Foreign Public Documents of Certification. This agreement is very important, it is about the Hague Convention as a major relief from full diplomatic certification. It concerns all international documents, and so the so-called Hague Apostille is there to confirm the authenticity of the signature, seal or stamp with which or with which a document is drawn. Further certification steps by competent Austrian authorities and additional control options are missing. This applies to all members of this multilateral agreement, and it is therefore so important. However, according to information from the Austrian Embassy in Manila, the document security in the Philippines is still lacking. Due to existing corruption, it should not be excluded that documents are issued with incorrect content. Therefore, it is intended to prevent the authorisation of Philippine documents as evidence without any further checks by Austrian authorities. This concerns mainly naturalisations, passport exhibitions and residence procedures of students in Austria. In fact, documents could be presented as evidence, which are actually issued, but whose content is wrong. In order to exclude this, Austria is thus opposed to the accession of the Philippines to the Hague apostille. Germany, the The Netherlands and Greece follow this opposition. - Thank you.

Point Report of the Committee on Research, Innovation and Digitalisation on the Government template (574 d.a._Other Organiser Federal Act, which lays down the Federal Law on Access to Websites and Mobile Applications of the Federal Law on Web Accessibility (WZG) (655 d.b.)
ParlaMint-AT_2019-07-03-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00086_d8e19165	In this way we reach the 27th place. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. Mrs Eva-Maria Himmelbauer. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2019-07-03-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00086_d8e19192	Madam President. Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! At this point, we are debating the Web Accessibility Act, which constitutes the implementation of the same directive. What's this about? – This law lays down the obligation and guarantee that public-hand websites and mobile applications are also accessible to people with disabilities. Technical specifications must be complied with in order to take account of the basic principles of accessibility such as perception, usability, understanding and robustness. But what does that actually mean in practice? – It is intended to address the needs of people who have visual, hearing impairment, impairment, for example also in the language field or a physical restriction. For example, you already know websites in which you can set the font size. There are often ash in smaller to large versions, which you can click on, and then the text is displayed larger or smaller. Or the text should be machine-readable so that blind people who use a special software for reading this text or converting this text into Braille can also understand the content of a website or application. It is therefore consistently a positive initiative and an important concern to provide all the relevant access to information and services of the federal, state and local authorities. This also fits seamlessly into the work of the previous Federal Government under Chancellor Sebastian Kurz and our digital minister Schramböck. Digitalisation has taken on a very important and high priority here, including the modernisation of public administration. It also includes facilitating communication and the provision of services for citizens and offering them in digital form – thank you very much – and this is independent and time-independent, so that people can really divide themselves when they want to use these services according to their needs and expectations. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP]] A milestone was the introduction of the Digital Office in March, which has been available since then and is a mobile service, where electoral cards can be requested or notifications can be made. This will be extended and extended in the future in order to bring together as many public authorities as possible in the interests of citizens in a mobile location. Of course, there has been a lot of know-how involved, and the employees of the Bundesrechenzentrum have brought in a lot of know-how, so I can express a warm thanks at this point. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] In order to return to the accessibility of mobile applications and websites, I would like to add that this should also take place in accordance with European standards. It is important for those involved in software development, in the web development field, that these standards have been defined by the specialist world involving the W3C, i.e. the World Wide Web Consortium. Any application, any website, among other things, must include a description of accessibility and, which is also very important, offer a contact possibility so that people who notice errors or failures can report them, so that they can then be enforced. One last point that the Austrian Disability Council has raised and which we have discussed in the Committee was that there are certain exceptions under the directive. These apply, for example, to archives, i.e. to obsolete contents that are still stored but are no longer processed, or to private internal networks, to which only a restricted user group has access. Finally, there is the exception of the directive for schools and kindergartens. Think of school websites in the compulsory school area or kindergarten websites. These are websites created by dedicated teachers in their spare time, which may also be provided by dedicated parents who have created them in their spare time, or which are created within the framework of projects by students. These are not professional websites, but this is not necessary for this purpose. It is usually more static websites that represent a little bit of the activities of the school. In any case, it is not about online management functions. We therefore use this derogation because we do not want to make it difficult for teachers, the parents, the students involved in the website, because they still have to implement it. I believe that exceptions to this extent are proportionate and, accordingly, justified. Overall, this is a very positive initiative, and I therefore ask for approval.

ParlaMint-AT_2019-07-03-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00086_d8e19660	Madam President. High house! The Web Accessibility Act has a difficult name, it has an important background. All of us – all authorities, all other public authorities – are increasingly using the Internet to provide a wide range of information and services that are of fundamental importance to the general public. The Web Accessibility Act aims to ensure that websites and mobile applications improve access on the basis of common requirements for accessibility. This concept of accessibility includes principles and techniques to be taken into account when designing, creating, maintaining and updating, in order to make content more accessible to users, especially those with disabilities. In technical terms, this Directive is currently considered to comply with Stage AA of the Accessibility-free Web Content Directive – WCAG 2.1. This will bring about the implementation of the EU's Web Accessibility Directive for the Federal Republic of Germany. The objectives we are pursuing are, in concrete terms, the better accessibility, better design and accessibility of websites and mobile Applications of the federal government and the possibility for users to indicate defects and thus enforce their elimination. As mentioned earlier, the Austrian Research Funding Society FFG is intended for the provision of these tasks under the Federal Law, in which the task of digitization was also anchored with the novelle of the FFG Act. Due to this very related subject matter, the tasks and functions under this federal law can and should be carried out in principle by the FFG, even if another competent body could be designated by regulation. In view of the general interest and the general importance of this issue, I should like to ask for broad support. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2019-07-03-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00086_d8e20091	Madam President. Mrs. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Article 9 of the UN Convention on the Rights of People with Disabilities obliges us – Austria – to take appropriate measures to ensure that people with disabilities have equal access to facilities and services open to or provided to the public. This must apply without limitation and without exceptions to all levels in federal, national and local authorities. Austria has also committed itself to implementing the WAI at EU level. In Section 1 para. 3 of the eGovernment Act is based on the need to implement access to public Internet access for people with disabilities. Now we have the Web Accessibility Act, which states that websites and mobile applications of the federal government have to meet the requirements of accessibility. Thank you for that, because this is a first step. At the same time, however, there are ten exceptions that are justified by the fact that they can be made with disproportionate effort. If such a one is given, you can make an exception. Why is it always an effort when it comes to people with disabilities? Why are we still standing here and discussing something that should be completely natural in our time and in our country? [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] Accessibility allows all people with and without disabilities the full use of services, facilities and objects in daily life. In the case of the right of equal treatment for persons with disabilities, this is the case if the use is quite normal, in a normal way, without any particular difficulty and in principle without any other assistance. Now quite honestly: I'm tired of this herumery when it comes to accessibility! [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] No one's helping on Sundays. Finally, let us insist that it is an actual right of all people, and especially of people with disabilities, to participate in life – in all areas and with all the means available to people without disabilities! Thank you.

Unfortunately, in recent years, fraud attempts in the theoretical driving test have increased again and again. New technologies, wider access to information about the Internet and smartphones not only facilitate our everyday and working world, but also create new opportunities for the disappearance of performance. With regard to driving licences, the organized crime seems to have discovered a new branch of business for itself. A mobile camera, which films the PC screen by means of a barely recognizable small hole in the pullover and thus the examination questions, is among other things the equipment of the fraudsters. Occasionally, however, a small camera integrated in glasses can also be used. This will transfer the questions to a helper outside the driving school. This, in turn, gives the correct answers via radio to a hearing aid hidden in the ear of the respective examiner. My Dear Ones However, ladies and gentlemen, the dizziness is not only very unfair to those who prepare diligently for such tests, but it also poses an unestimateable safety risk for the general public in the area of steering rights if driver's license holders do not know and control our road traffic rules. As we all know, driving is a potentially dangerous activity. The less the knowledge of road users about traffic regulations is, the more accidents and in the worst case deaths we have to fear. It is therefore time to establish a legal basis for the sanctioning of driving licence fraud in order to ensure that the approximately 210 000 driver's licence tests taking place annually in Austria, of which 85 000 at least fall within Class B, are properly placed in order to maintain road safety. ==References== FSG-Novelle we create a legal basis with an eye-sized view. In future, those examiners who are caught in the highly technized swindle à la James Bond will be subjected to a nine-month blocking period for re-entry. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen, this innovation is very important, it can save lives, as it is not a cavalier offence, just like alcohol in road transport. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2019-07-03-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00086_d8e22035	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! First of all, I would like to thank you for supporting the innovation of the Railway Act. There is the separation of sales and infrastructure in all areas, there is no cross-financing, and we are internationally at the highest level. On the topic of expanding the rail, of course, a clear commitment from my side: When we look at the figures, we see that we are also very good in international comparison. Every Austrian, every Austrian rides an average of 1,500 kilometers per year by train. We transport 260 million passengers per year with the ÖBB – which is really a top value in international comparison with the size of our country – and we invest more than 2 billion euros each year in the expansion, improvement, modernisation and maintenance of this infrastructure. There are, of course, clear priorities as measured by the potential passenger volume et cetera. So we simply have these expansion programmes, and we cannot expand everything at the same time, although, of course, we understand these applications, which come from certain regions. We are always looking for contact, but we also need to understand that we have to set priorities. With regard to the motion for a resolution on public tickets, I would like to state in the final analysis that I see this in two parts from my wait. The first priority is the creation of a single distribution system. This sounds technically relatively simple, but the greater challenge in the distribution system is the billing behind it – with the transport associations, with the various transport service providers. This story, if you look at the landscape in this area, is not quite that trivial. We're working on it, we're on a good way. I also have a very high priority, but it takes some time. The second issue is the setting-up of tariffs, and we simply have to figure out which tariffs are available. It is also a question of subsidising. Only when you have this billing system can you be serious about what such a ticket can cost, and from that you can make a plan and also negotiate with the Minister of Finance in which dimension you need to subsidise it.

The purpose of this measure is to avoid unwarranted ads in the course of fire brigade operations, because the vehicles are clearly identified as vehicles of the firearm in the future. This scheme applies only to transport within the Austrian federal territory. This new regulation is intended to benefit our domestic companies and facilitate their transport routes. In the future, passenger cars will also be allowed to carry on a trailer in mobile crane weights. Ladies and gentlemen! This is not the most exciting legislative innovation we are deciding today in this House, but it is undoubtedly an important catalogue of legal innovations to make the KFG more practical, comprehensible and a lot more just.
